Adds an additive `economicContextBonus` scoring term (settlement boom/bust × template-family affinity from a new `ECONOMIC_SCENE_AFFINITY` data table — no `UnifiedActionTemplate` change, dodging the 291-importer blast radius), cause-agnostic shock seeding in `phaseProsperity` (an `econLastProsperity` baseline catches the divine verbs' *direct* property writes, not only queued `ProsperityShock`s, so battle aftermath and any future cause qualify through the same test), `{econ_adj}`/`{econ_noun}`/`{econ_atmosphere}` prose coloration, and a DebugPanel breakdown line + `econ_shock_seeded` trace. Two divergences from the plan doc recorded and forced by measuring against the live registry (audit-on-touch): the economic scene ids live under `encounter.*` not the plan's family names, and family-stub seeds would draw any of the 171 `encounter.*` templates — so affinity keys became longest-prefix full ids and seeds name explicit templates. 55 new tests incl. a registry-tying contract suite. Registry rows `economy-context-scene-scoring` + `economy-verbs-answered` → 🟢 LIVE with 120-tick evidence (184 nonzero contributions) + a blight→shock→scene end-to-end trace. Suite 12886 green, typecheck ratchet 3525 = 3525, `vite build` green, 30-tick smoke clean, browser `__DEBUG` confirmed the term flows live (`encounter.aid_refugees econ=0.107`). Closes via `Fixes THR-725`.

✅ **THR-714 — Deterministic `ul-dashboard.generated.json` regeneration** (2026-07-23, Continuous Improvement) — The generated UL-dashboard snapshot carried a wall-clock `generatedAt`. `check:generated-freshness` ignored it as volatile, but **git does not**: any two PRs that each ran `npm run prebuild` held different blobs of the same file, so under strict branch protection every merge in a queued cascade turned the remaining PRs `DIRTY` — a real content conflict, not `BEHIND` — and `gh pr update-branch` no-ops on `DIRTY`, so armed auto-merges silently never fired and the queue stalled with zero failure signal (impediment #197: two stalled PRs #740/#742 and ~25 min of manual conflict surgery on 2026-07-22). Fixed at the source rather than in the gate: `generatedAt` removed from `ULDashboardData` and from the emitted JSON, so identical shard sources produce a byte-identical artifact (NFP #3 applied to a generated file). `VOLATILE_JSON_KEYS` is now **empty** — kept as a mechanism with a note that the carve-out only ever hid the problem from *this* gate, and that a deterministic generator is the right fix for the next one. The dashboard footer showed "Generated &lt;timestamp&gt;"; it now shows "Schema v1 · 96 terms" — a timestamp never proved the snapshot current, since `prebuild` refreshes it on every build. Two regression tests replace the old equal-modulo-`generatedAt` assertion: byte-identical serialization across runs, and no wall-clock key in the output. **Predicate sweep** (per the ticket's "any generated artifact whose regen output differs with unchanged inputs"): ran `prebuild` twice and diffed — this was the only non-deterministic artifact of the 25 checked; action-catalog, impediment-dashboard and every design-wiki page were already byte-stable, so there is nothing else to strip. Full suite **12815 green (855 files)**, typecheck ratchet **3529 = 3529** (zero net-new), `vite build` green, and `check:generated-freshness` now reports `OK — 25 generated artifact(s) match HEAD` leaving a **clean working tree** (previously it left the dashboard modified every run). Browser-verified at `?view=ul`. Closes via `Fixes THR-714`.

✅ **THR-573 — Encounter context multiplication: Tier-2 surface generators** (2026-07-23, Content Architecture) — The *reading* half of surface multiplication. Phase 0 (THR-475) shipped the naming half: `computeSurfaceKey` already treats the same template at a guild vs a shrine vs a road as different surfaces for novelty and recency — but the prose did not follow, so those "different" surfaces read identically and the multiplication was an accounting trick the player could see through. **Grammar:** *identity* axes create surfaces (`place` = `sublocationTypeId`, `counterpartRole` = `npcRole` — exactly the per-template levers already in `SURFACE_KEY_AXES`, so prose identity and selection identity cannot drift apart); *coloration* axes (sphere/omen vocabulary, `{cast:*}` continuity, `{intel:*}`, relationship forks) vary the reading for free but never count toward the library. **Engine:** new pure `src/engine/fragmentResolution.ts` — `resolveFragment` (bound value → `'*'` default → strip + warn **once per template**, never per render), `resolveTemplateFragments`, `enumerateTemplateSurfaces`; additive `ContextFragmentSet` + optional `contextFragments?` on the 278-importer `UnifiedActionTemplate` with zero signature changes; `enrichProse` gains `{frag:<slot>}` resolved **first**, before every other token, so `{name}`/`{target}`/`{cast:*}` *inside* a fragment resolve exactly as if written inline; the two identity axes are derived inside `gatherNarrativeContext` from state it already resolves, so no caller threads a new required parameter and absent context is the `'*'` path by design. **Zero PRNG** — resolution is a pure lookup, same surface same words (NFP #3 for free); `FRAGMENT_SEED_OFFSET` reserved but unused for a future multi-variant path. `surface_fragments_bound` trace emitted once per encounter *instantiation*, never per step render (ring-buffer discipline). **Content:** `social_scene.recruitment_pitch` carries 9 fragments → **20 authored surfaces** (5 places × 4 roles), all scoring `pass` / score 100 / zero flags under the THR-609 register scorer. Two silent-no-op traps found and closed: `toSocialTemplate` is an explicit field **whitelist** (an unlisted field is dropped without error), and `narrativeTemplates.initiation` is read raw by Codex/preview surfaces that never run `enrichProse` (a bare token would leak to the player — new `staticProse()` expands it to the default). **UI:** new Fragments debug tab (static inventory + live bindings with a `usedDefault` column) and `__DEBUG.resolveSurfaceFragments(ref?)`; the Phase-0 surfaceKey display the plan referenced did not exist, so a dedicated tab was built. **Measurement:** `npm run volume-model` reports measured authored counts beside the arithmetic targets (byte-identical across runs, hash-verified), making the ~1,000 target an observable. **Authoring:** new `template-context-rewrite` skill (four passes); `encounter-pipeline` deliberately unchanged. 33 new tests; full suite **12814 green (855 files)**, typecheck ratchet **3529 = 3529** (zero net-new), `vite build` green, 30-tick CLI smoke clean (389 agents, 51 events/tick), browser-verified at the Fragments tab with a clean console. **Deviation from plan, stated:** the plan's 9 fragments used `{actor}`, which `enrichProse` does not resolve on the encounter step-prose path — confirmed rendering literally to the player — so they were landed with `{name}` per the plan's own authoring rule; the pre-existing file-wide leak is impediment #198 / THR-716. Not organically observed: `social_scene.*` was never selected in 80-tick smoke windows, so the trace's emission was proven by contract test rather than a live firing. Closes via `Fixes THR-573`.

✅ **THR-697 — Scene Integration Slice D: seed system v2 (family matching + context inheritance)** (2026-07-22, Content Architecture) — Two structural gaps in `encounterSeeding.ts` closed. **(1) Family matching activated.** 86 of 186 authored `encounter_seed` effects are family-only, and the v1 stub only emitted an advisory "a … encounter may surface soon" narrative event — half the planted future stories were theater. `evaluateEncounterSeeds` now resolves a family-only seed to a concrete template: candidates are registered `UNIFIED_ACTION_TEMPLATES` whose `id` starts with `${family}.` (the THR-112 `revealFamilies` prefix convention — **no separate family registry**), filtered to individual-performable templates (`actorAffinities` includes `'individual'`) whose `locationSubtypes` restriction (if any) matches the target agent's current location subtype (unrestricted templates always pass); the scan collects up to `FAMILY_SEED_MAX_CANDIDATES` (12) eligibles, then makes exactly one seeded `rng()` draw and spawns the winner through the same lifecycle as a direct-`templateId` seed (busy check, causation fields, "planted thread bears fruit" TickEvent with the matched template name appended). Zero eligible → the v1 withered narrative event, preserved byte-identical. **(2) Context inheritance (opt-in).** Additive `inheritContext?: boolean` on the `encounter_seed` effect + `inheritedTargetId?`/`inheritedBindings?` on `PendingEncounterSeed` (both `unifiedAction.ts`, additive). When set, the plant site in `applyEncounterAftermathReaction` snapshots the source action's `targetId` and `supportBindings` onto the seed; at spawn `resolveSeedInheritance` re-validates both against the live graph — a dead inherited target falls back to self-target (today's behavior), dead-node bindings are dropped — and threads the survivors into the spawned action's normal `supportBindings` slot, so Slice A/C `{target:*}`/`{cast:*}` placeholders and Slice B `$target`/`$cast:` aftermath sentinels resolve to the original scene in the follow-up. **The same people return** (forge-alliance's "ally calls in a favor" seed finally stars the ally, not a stranger). Two typed traces `encounter_seed_family_matched` (family draw resolved a template) + `seed_context_inherited` (inheritance re-validated at spawn) added to `TraceEntry` and emitted via the Slice-B Omit-collapse cast. New constant `FAMILY_SEED_MAX_CANDIDATES` (`effect-constants.ts`). No new node/edge type, GameState top-level field, orchestrator phase, or UI component. Wiring guide Capability 2 rewritten for family-v2 + `inheritContext`; `wiring-checklist.md` gains the THR-697 note (reference HTML seed rules N/A — they never described the v1 stub). 12 new tests in `encounterSeeding.test.ts` (family draw + determinism, inheritance carry + dead-node drop + dead-target fallback, plant-site snapshot both ways, withered-no-eligible regression); full suite **12676 green (841 files)**, typecheck ratchet **3529 = 3529** (zero net-new; THR-489), `vite build` green, 30-tick CLI smoke clean (389 agents, 57 events/tick) + a live CLI demo (planted a `broker.quest` family-only seed → tick 1 spawned `broker.quest.rival_shrine_betrayal`, seed consumed). Engine-only; reuses the existing TickEvent → browser-verify exempt. Blocked-by THR-695 (Done). Closes via `Fixes THR-697`.

✅ **THR-695 — Scene Integration Slice B: aftermath scene sentinels + `bond_change`** (2026-07-22, Content Architecture) — Aftermath effects resolved `targetAgentId` as a literal node id; `$target` existed only for the three THR-555 reach-signature effects, and the wiring guide's `role:` prefix had **no resolver anywhere in the engine** (the three `src/data/encounters/examples/` files silently fell back to the actor). Slice B closes both gaps and adds the first authored effect that can *create* a relationship. **Sentinel bind pass:** `bindAftermathSceneTargets(effect, action, graph, traceCtx?)` runs at the top of the aftermath dispatch loop, composed **after** `bindReachSignatureTargets` so `$primary` + the three signature kinds keep their behavior. It rebinds sentinel values on `targetAgentId`/`targetFactionId`/`targetSublocationId`/`withAgentId`: `'$target'` → `action.targetId` **only when the resolved node kind matches the field** (`nodeMatchesSceneField` — an agent field pointed at a location is left unbound → existing invalid-target no-op), and `'$cast:<key>'` / legacy alias `'role:<key>'` → the `action.supportBindings` entry for that key. Unresolvable sentinels stay in place (fail-soft, NFP #4); every processed sentinel emits one `aftermath_sentinel_bound` trace (`resolvedNodeId: null` when unbound). The pass is effect-kind-agnostic (operates on field names), so `role:victim` on `hidden_mark`/`apply_condition`/`reputation_score` all resolve — the three example files are now genuinely functional once the action supplies the matching bindings. **`bond_change` effect kind** (new member of the 278-importer `EncounterAftermathReactionEffect` union in `unifiedAction.ts`, additive-only): `applyBondEdge` creates or mutates the actor→`withAgentId` `relates_to` edge — creating at `BOND_CREATE_INITIAL_SENTIMENT`/`BOND_CREATE_INITIAL_TRUST` when absent — applies `sentimentDelta` (clamped [-1,1]) and optional `trustDelta` (clamped [0,1]), mirrors the same deltas onto the reverse edge when `reciprocal !== false` (default true), and calls `touchWorld()`. `withAgentId` accepts a literal id, `$target`, or `$cast:`/`role:` (bound by the sentinel pass). Fail-soft no-op (`success:false` trace) on an unresolved sentinel, a non-actor node, or a self-bond; emits `bond_change_applied` + the standard `encounter_aftermath_effect` trace. Both traces are typed interfaces added to `TraceEntry` and emitted via the established Omit-collapse cast (`as unknown as Parameters<typeof emitTrace>[0]`). No new node or edge type. New constants `BOND_CREATE_INITIAL_SENTIMENT`/`_TRUST` (`effect-constants.ts`). Wiring guide Part 5 multi-target note rewritten to the sentinel vocabulary + a `bond_change` effect-types row + a trace-categories row; `wiring-checklist.md` gains the THR-695 block. Engine-only — content adoption of `bond_change`/sentinels is Slice F (THR-699); browser-verify exempt (engine-only, unit + snapshot cover). **Out of scope per the plan's explicit 4-field list:** `witnessAgentIds` array-level `role:` on `recent_event` stays literal (a follow-up if wanted). 19-test regression lock in `src/engine/__tests__/encounterAftermath-bond-scene.test.ts`; 12668 tests green (841 files), typecheck ratchet **3529 = 3529** (zero net-new; THR-489), `vite build` green, 30-tick CLI smoke clean (389 agents, 57 events/tick). Slice B **blocks** D + F. Closes via `Fixes THR-695`.

✅ **THR-694 — Scene Integration Slice A: `{target:*}` enrichment placeholders** (2026-07-22, Content Architecture) — Encounters bind a real `action.targetId` (another agent or a location) and then wrote "the other party" — `NarrativeContext` was 100% actor-anchored, so prose could not name the entity the scene was *with*. Slice A makes the target a first-class prose subject and is the foundation of the A–F chain (it **blocks** C and F). Engine: new `SceneTargetContext` + `NarrativeContext.target`, and `resolveSceneTargetContext(graph, actorId, targetId)` — agent-kind targets carry pronouns, faction name, and an actor→target `relation` classified via the **reused** ±0.35 `ALLY_SENTIMENT_THRESHOLD`/`ENEMY_SENTIMENT_THRESHOLD` (no new tunable; absence of a `relates_to` edge → `stranger`); location-kind targets carry name only; the block is omitted for self-targeted actions (`targetId === actorId`), deleted nodes, and callers passing no targetId. `gatherNarrativeContext` gained a trailing `opts?: { targetId?: string }`; the two encounter-path callers (`buildUnifiedEncounterStageModel`, `unifiedActionResolution`) pass `action.targetId`. **Foreshadowing legitimately does not** — `BalanceEncounterPoolCandidate` carries only a `locationId`, no bound target, so its block stays absent, which is the correct read for a not-yet-realized encounter. `enrichProse` gained `{target}` (→ "the other party" fallback), `{target:they|them|their|s}` (+capitalized, neutral fallback), `{target:faction}` (→ "their people"), and the conditionals `{?has_target}`/`{?no_target}` + `{?target_is_ally|rival|stranger}` — every token fail-soft so absence reads as absence and never invents a referent; unknown `{target:*}` tokens are residually stripped. A location-kind target is present (`has_target` true) but relation-less, so all three `target_is_*` resolve false for it. **`window.__DEBUG.inspectSceneContext(agentRef)`** is the DoD state-assertion hook for the whole chain, returning `{ actionId, templateId, targetId, targetName, targetKind, relation, bindings[] }`. Engine-only — content adoption is Slice F (THR-699); **zero single-brace `{target}` tokens exist in any enrichProse-processed authored content yet** (the 348 `{{target}}` hits are the disjoint double-brace action-template resolver — proven disjoint because `enrichProse` already replaces `{their}` alongside `{{their}}` content with no collision), so there is no visible UI delta. 12649 tests green (840 files), typecheck ratchet **3529 = 3529** (zero net-new; THR-489), `vite build` green, 30-tick CLI smoke clean (389 agents, 57 events, 18 unified actions). Regression locks in `src/engine/__tests__/proseEnrichment.test.ts` (68 tests). Browser-verify exempt: engine-only slice, no render-structure change, no authored content uses the tokens yet. Closes via `Fixes THR-694`.

✅ **THR-691 — union merge for closeout docs, and the honest half of the answer** (2026-07-21, Continuous Improvement) — Every shipped ticket appends rows to the same three table heads, so a PR that idles is *structurally* guaranteed to conflict with the next ship in closeout docs alone — THR-668's sat ~31 h and went `CONFLICTING` with zero code conflicts, burning a run on hand-resolution, and human-gated PRs idle **by design**. `.gitattributes` now marks `Docs/changelog.md`, `Docs/project-history.md`, and `Docs/impediments.md` `merge=union`, with `Docs/project-status.md` deliberately excluded (60-line cap + rewrite semantics — union would duplicate rewritten lines, the second-order trap the THR-668 resolution itself hit). **The first demo proved nothing and was redone.** A single union merge exiting 0 is not evidence: git's `ort` strategy often merges adjacent insertions cleanly on its own, so the result was consistent with the attribute doing nothing. The control was then botched a second time — resetting onto a branch that had *already* absorbed the merge produced "Already up to date", a green result measuring nothing. The valid A/B builds two branch pairs from bases differing *only* in the attribute and inserts conflicting rows at the same line: **without union, exit 1 with conflict markers; with union, exit 0, both rows present, table header count still 1.** **The ticket's optimistic premise was wrong, and measuring it was the deliverable.** Scope 1 supposed that `union`, being a *built-in* driver rather than a custom one, might be respected by GitHub's server-side merge. It is not. Probed against real pushed branches with the attribute live on the base: `POST /repos/.../merges` returned **409 Merge conflict**, and a PR of the same shape read **`mergeable: CONFLICTING` / `mergeStateStatus: DIRTY`** — the exact THR-668 symptom. GitHub's merge machinery does not consult `.gitattributes` at all. So the honest deliverable is the documented fallback the ticket named as its contingency, not a silent partial fix: `pull-work` gains a closeout section stating the measurement and prescribing `git fetch origin main && git merge origin/main && git push`, with the caveat that union keeps **both** sides of every conflicting hunk — correct for an appended row, wrong for an edited one, so the diff wants a skim for duplicates rather than trust in the clean exit. CLAUDE.md § Definition of Done carries the one-line pointer. Net effect: local merges of stale closeout branches stop needing hand-resolution; GitHub-side conflicts still need a local merge, but now there is a named procedure instead of a per-run rediscovery. The probe branches and PR were deleted immediately after measurement; PRs into a non-`main` base trigger no CI, so the empirical check cost zero Actions minutes. 12633 tests green (840 files), `vite build` green. Browser-verify exempt: git config + docs only, zero `src/` files touched. Closes via `Fixes THR-691`.

✅ **THR-690 — generated artifacts can no longer ship stale** (2026-07-21, Continuous Improvement) — `public/action-catalog.generated.json` is consumed at runtime but was rebuilt only by the npm `prebuild` lifecycle hook. **The ticket's premise needed correcting on contact:** `prebuild` *already* chained `generate-action-catalog` (since THR-519), so scope item 1 was done — the actual hole was that both CI's build step and CLAUDE.md's documented pre-commit gate invoke `npx vite build`, which bypasses npm lifecycle hooks entirely, so the chain never ran where it mattered. Proven directly: `npx vite build` against a scratch template edit left the stale catalog byte-identical. That is why two consecutive runs (THR-616, THR-663) nearly leaked, caught only by agent vigilance. **Two artifacts were already stale on `main` when the work started** — `Design/impediment-dashboard.html` ~848 lines behind `Docs/impediments.md`, and `src/data/ul-dashboard.generated.json` carrying a pre-design-session `Implementation Plan` UL body — committed first so the new gate is green on arrival. **The ticket's proposed implementation would not have worked.** Scope item 3 specified `git diff --exit-code -- '*generated*'`; `ul-dashboard.generated.json` embeds a wall-clock `generatedAt` that the UL dashboard renders as "Generated <date>", so that assertion would have been red on every run regardless of freshness. The gate normalizes declared volatile keys per path and asserts *content* freshness instead. **A second design was built and discarded mid-run:** inferring the artifact set by diffing the dirty tree before and after `prebuild` is self-maintaining, but silently passes when the artifact is already regenerated-but-uncommitted (before and after hashes match, so it is never compared to `HEAD`) — a false pass in exactly the local workflow the gate protects. The shipped version compares an explicit artifact set (3 static paths + the 22 design-wiki pages enumerated from `wiki-manifest.json`, so adding a page needs no code change) **and** fails when `prebuild` writes a path outside that set, which closes the forgotten-registration hole the discarded design was reaching for. Deliberately not fail-soft: NFP #4 governs the tick loop, where a throw costs the player their run; a build gate that passes when it cannot verify is the theater THR-686 named. Scope 4 re-points `check:wiki-freshness` at generated payloads via a new `payloads` manifest field — `action-catalog.html` is a shell whose content lives in the JSON, so payload-only edits read as staleness (impediment #180's root: freshness keyed on the wrong artifact). Verified load-bearing by removing the declaration (WARN) and restoring it (OK). Five behaviours proven end to end: red on genuinely stale artifacts (caught both real ones), green when only `generatedAt` moves, red on a template edit whose catalog was not committed, **red on that same case when the catalog was already regenerated-but-uncommitted** (the discarded design's false pass), and unrelated uncommitted edits never reported. 12633 tests green (840 files), `vite build` green, `tsc -b --force` 3529 = 3529 — zero net-new. Browser-verify exempt: build tooling. Closes via `Fixes THR-690`.

✅ **THR-689 — `__DEBUG.tick(n)`, a headless tick bridge** (2026-07-21, Continuous Improvement) — Automated browser tabs report `document.hidden`, which throttles the interval-driven tick loop to roughly one tick per interaction. Any Done-when of the form "run N ticks, then observe X" was therefore unreachable *by construction*: THR-644's 40-tick reproduction had to fall back to a headless CLI sweep, which exercises engine state but never the real UI render path. `window.__DEBUG.tick(n)` drives the same `runTick` pipeline the auto-play loop calls, synchronously, bypassing frame scheduling entirely. **The naive implementation would have silently failed.** Calling the existing `doTick()` n times in a loop does not advance n ticks — `doTick` reads `gameStateRef.current`, which React only refreshes on render, so every iteration ticks from the same `prev` and the counter moves by exactly 1 while the graph accumulates partial side effects. `runTickBatch` keeps its own cursor, commits once with `setGameState`, and updates the ref before committing so a second call in the same task starts from the advanced state. Guard rails, each a named constant or explicit branch: `DEBUG_TICK_MAX = 200` **clamps** an oversized request rather than rejecting it (`capped: true`, so a typo still yields a usable result instead of an untouched sim); the run loop auto-pauses, since leaving the interval armed would let it resume mid-inspection from the state just advanced; a throw inside any tick stops the batch and returns partial progress with `stoppedReason: 'error'` rather than propagating (NFP #4 — a debug helper must never be the thing that crashes the loop); and exactly **one** aggregate `debug_tick_batch` trace is emitted per call, never one per tick, because a 200-tick per-tick burst would evict most of a run's real traces from the 2000-entry ring buffer. Logic lives in `src/engine/debugTickBatch.ts` — React-free on purpose, so tests drive it directly against real `initializeGameState` → `runTick` rather than through `renderHook`. Seven regression tests cover the cap (bounded by an independent witness: the per-tick resolver's call count), the one-trace-per-call rule, tick-delta accuracy, phase-exit, non-positive input, and the fail-soft path. One test assertion was corrected mid-run rather than papered over: the cap test initially asserted `ticksRun === 200` and failed at 185, because on that seed/map the doom clock legitimately ends the run first — asserting equality would have coupled a guard-rail test to doom-clock pacing, so it now asserts the actual guarantee (`≤ DEBUG_TICK_MAX`). **Verified from an automated tab with `document.hidden === true`** (reproduced in both the Browser pane and real Chrome): tick 1 → 41, delta exactly 40, `stoppedReason: 'completed'`; UI re-rendered to `tick 41 · spring · year 1` with World Pulse `Tick 41 / Active Agents 399` and Chronicle entries at T41/T40 — real simulated history, not a counter bump — and a `paused` indicator confirming auto-pause. 12633 tests green (840 files), `vite build` green, 30-tick CLI smoke green (389 agents). Types: `tsc -b --force` **3529 = 3529** with and without the change — zero net-new; the apparent diff is line-shift and union-ellipsis churn from adding a `TraceEntry` member, each "new" error pairing with a "resolved" one at an offset matching the insertions exactly. CLAUDE.md's standing constraint is lifted from "no N-tick browser Done-when until THR-689" to "only via `__DEBUG.tick(n)`" — Play-button ticking in an automated tab remains unreachable. Closes via `Fixes THR-689`.

✅ **THR-686 — The advisory gates stop lying** (2026-07-21, Continuous Improvement) — Four honest-signal fixes bundled on one theme: gates that lie train agents to bypass gates (`--no-verify` appeared twice in the last 12 runs, each time justified by a false positive). **(1) `tsc --noEmit` theater retired.** Root `tsconfig.json` sets `files: []`, so `npx tsc --noEmit` exits 0 unconditionally no matter how broken the code is — verified directly — yet six live process surfaces listed it as a real gate and every run cited its exit 0 as evidence. CLAUDE.md (three sites), `AGENTS.md` (two), `Docs/canon/process.md`, `Docs/plans/_template.md`, `Docs/ops/cc-hourly-opus-pickup-prompt.md`, and the live `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/tb-opus-pickup/SKILL.md` now say plainly that CI's `Test · Typecheck · Build` is the authoritative type gate and that local evidence is a `tsc -b --force` **net-new** diff against the red ~3.5k baseline (THR-489), not an absolute count. Historical plan docs were deliberately left alone — they are records of what was run, and rewriting them would be revisionism. **(2) `lint:plan-doc` false findings fixed — and the ticket's suggested fix would not have worked.** The proposal was to scope the lint to dated `Docs/plans/20??-??-??-*.md`, but the canonical offender, `2026-04-16-systemic-wiring-guide.md`, is *itself* date-prefixed, so date-scoping would still have linted it and still emitted all 31 findings. Implemented the ticket's second option instead: a `> **lint_plan_doc:** exempt` frontmatter marker, scanned only in the first 15 lines so a prose mention of the rule cannot silently exempt a real plan doc, fail-soft to not-exempt on an unreadable file. The wiring guide declares itself a standing reference; verified 31 → 0 on it while a real dated plan doc still reports its 31. **(3) `check:process` side-effect root-caused.** The check mutated tracked `Docs/authoring-brief.md` mid-run, which had broken a `git stash pop`. `check-authoring-brief.ts` is itself read-only and `build-authoring-brief.ts` carried a correct `import.meta.url === process.argv[1]` entry-point guard — the defect was that **esbuild `--bundle` defeats that guard**: the check bundles the generator into its *own* entry file, rewriting `import.meta.url` to point at `.cache/check-authoring-brief.mjs`, which *is* `process.argv[1]`, so the guard evaluated true and the generator ran. Confirmed by reading the emitted bundle. Fixed by gating on the entry file's basename, which bundling preserves. Verified with the ticket's own predicate: `check:process` run against a dirty tree leaves `git status` byte-identical, while now correctly *warning* about brief drift instead of silently fixing it — and the generator still writes when invoked directly. **(4) `/pull-work` Step 1 rewritten.** The prescribed single `list_issues(limit:250)` board scan returns ~390k characters and is rejected outright on response size, so the canonical pickup path failed at step one and every run improvised. Step 1 and the `pullNextReadyForDev` wrapper now specify two state-filtered calls (In Dev; Ready for Dev + `assignee:null`), sorted by priority in memory — matching what `keep-work-flowing-cc` § 1 already documented, so the two skills stop contradicting each other. The same broken instruction was also live in **both** pickup-prompt copies (repo + scheduled task) and was fixed in both. This run's own pickup used the two-call scan end to end. 12625 of 12626 tests green (839 files); the single failure is `chapterArchive.test.ts` timing out at 5s under full-suite load, **proven pre-existing and unrelated** — it passes in 1.2s in isolation and this change touches zero `src/` files (THR-489 baseline). `vite build` green. Types: `tsc -b --force` **3529 = 3529** with and without the change — zero net-new, measured by the very method this ticket documents. Browser-verify exempt: tooling and docs only, no runtime UI. Closes via `Fixes THR-686`.

✅ **THR-685 — The two-tree edit-path trap gets a mechanical guard** (2026-07-21, Continuous Improvement) — In a worktree session, an `Edit`/`Write` carrying a bare repo-root absolute path lands in the **home** tree and *succeeds*: the trees are byte-identical at branch time, so `old_string` matches and nothing errors. Verification then runs against unedited code. It fired in **4 of 12 hourly runs** on 2026-07-20/21, three consecutive (impediments #387, #417, #421), and the worst instance was a green signal that actively concealed the miss — `check:skill-sync:sync` reporting "Synced 41 shared skills" while mirroring the unmodified copy. Three impediment entries had already asked for a standing check; skill text alone never stuck, which is why this ships a **PreToolUse hook** (`worktree-write-guard.sh`) as the durable half. The guard derives both roots from git rather than hardcoding a path — session root from `--show-toplevel`, home tree from the parent of `--git-common-dir`, which in a linked worktree points at the main repo's `.git` — so it covers `.claude/worktrees/*` and sibling `tfws-pickup-*` alike. Two ordering details are load-bearing: it gates on **CWD** (an interactive home-tree session writing home-tree files is legitimate and must never be blocked) and it tests the worktree prefix **before** the home prefix, because a `.claude/worktrees/*` root is lexically *under* the home tree and the naive order would block every legitimate write. Every probe fail-softs to allow; a guard that errors is a guard that stops real work. Blocked calls print the corrected worktree-prefixed path. Scope items 1–2 add the prose half to both `pull-work` and the hourly `tb-opus-pickup` prompt: write-path discipline at session start, and a home-tree cleanliness gate (`git -C <home> status --porcelain`) before the closing commit, with recovery pointing at impediments #417/#387 rather than duplicating it. Done-when demonstrated with the *same* target path yielding exit 2 from a worktree CWD and exit 0 from a home CWD, plus backslash and mixed-separator variants (Windows paths arrive both ways). 12626 tests green (839 files), `tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `vite build` green. Browser-verify exempt: infra only, no `src/` touched. **Known limit, stated rather than papered over:** the hook covers `Edit`/`Write` only — `Bash` redirects and `cp` into the home tree remain the agent's responsibility, and hooks load at session start, so the guard arms for the *next* session, not the one that wrote it.

✅ **THR-654 — Cowork demolition: one skill tree, no flush pipeline, five impediment classes structurally closed** (2026-07-21, Pure Claude Code Migration) — Phase 3 of the migration, removing the machinery that existed only to bridge two runtimes. Deleted: the `flush-plan-docs` skill, the `plan-pending-commit` label and every live reference to it, `.github/workflows/flush-close-guard.yml` (built to intercept auto-flush branches tripping Linear's native close keywords — a threat vector that dies with the branches), the entire `.agents/skills/` duplicate tree (180 files), `scripts/check-skill-sync.js`, the THR-192 pre-commit hook line, and the `check:skill-sync*` npm scripts. CLAUDE.md's "Cowork vs Claude Code" section became **"Session Types: Design vs Execution"** — the split is now a session type, not a runtime — with the Cowork-specific sandbox limitations dropped, Skill Tree Layout collapsed to a single tree with a do-not-reintroduce note, and the Session Workflow / Definition of Done flush steps rewritten to the direct `docs/plan-*` PR flow. Same pass through `Docs/canon/process.md` (incl. two new Rejected Approaches), `canon/README.md`, the coordination protocol, the `Plan doc` UL entry, `wiring-checklist.md`, the hourly pickup prompt (its whole "1.5 Flush pending plan docs" step is gone), and four skills. `AGENTS.md` — which still described the **Codex** lane retired back in June — was reduced to a pointer at CLAUDE.md rather than maintained as a second divergent protocol copy. **The deletion exposed a failure class the sync hook structurally could not catch: cross-tree pointers.** The hook compared same-named skills for drift; it had nothing to say about a `.claude/` file whose body reads "load the `.agents/` version for execution." `attachment-pipeline` was exactly that stub — and the delegation was false in both directions: the `.agents` copy was a **33-line subset** of the fuller `.claude` file, and the four `agents/*-prompt.md` files it advertised have **never existed at any commit**, so the skill has been promising a 4-pass pipeline with nothing behind it (THR-684). Two `design-council` role prompts needed plain path rewrites. Verification separately caught `npm run drift-scan` dead on startup — extensionless `scripts/lint-rulebook` import — **proven pre-existing** by reverting the local edit and reproducing byte-identically, which raises the question of whether the weekly Friday drift-scan Action that feeds the retrospective has been silently failing (THR-683). Impediment log gains a structural-closure section for five classes — skill-sync false positives (~46 recurrences, 62 mentions), flush false-closes (#162), mount corruption (#11), Obsidian-MCP-unreachable (#66/71/75/86), Linear-auth-in-Cowork (#182/183) — **append-only, nothing above rewritten**; THR-540 closes as moot since the script it would have fixed is gone. 12626 tests green (839 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` green. Browser-verify exempt: no `src/` file touched. **Landed only after Christian's plain-language sign-off**, per the standing intent-judge condition on the Allow verdict.

✅ **THR-643 — Motive-receipt foreshadowing Phase D: the capability gets documented before content authors need it** (2026-07-21, Thematic Pressure & Living World) — Docs-only split off THR-631, whose code had already shipped across PRs #541/#544/#545/#547 while the authoring surfaces stayed undocumented. Three surfaces gained the Motive Receipt: the **systemic wiring guide** (new Capability 11 — receipt fields, the S1–S4 clause slots, determinism seed, fail-soft rejection, authored-override precedence), the **`prose-content-systems` skill** (new System 11 — typed slots with worked ✅/❌ examples for the two bugs the realizer exists to prevent, plus the requirement to register any new clause pool in the sweep's `RECEIPT_CLAUSES` or it ships unchecked), and the **wiring checklist** (full module/property/trace/debug table). The pass also found the existing THR-389 docs materially stale and corrected them in place — the resolver had been renamed, types had moved, `genericFallback.ts` was deprecated, and both docs still claimed the foreshadowing signals were Phase-1 stubs (`intelligenceTier` "always `'unknown'`") when they have been derived from real agent state since Phase B. Verified `npm test` **12623 passed (839 files)** and `vite build` green; no source file was touched, so typecheck and build were unaffected by construction. Browser-verify exempt: docs-only, no runtime UI change. THR-631 stays **In Dev** for the remaining UI slice.

✅ **THR-673 — Reaper hardening: the silent failure explained, hourly cadence, stale-worktree escalation — and a live-session-destroying bug caught in verification** (2026-07-21, Continuous Improvement) — The `-2147020576` refusals had a mundane cause: Task Scheduler power management was set to `Stop On Battery Mode, No Start On Batteries`, so every attempt made on battery was refused and wrote **nothing** to the log — indistinguishable from "the task never fired". Fixed with `AllowStartIfOnBatteries`, `DontStopIfGoingOnBatteries`, and `StartWhenAvailable` (the last also covering the 07-06→07-17 machine-off gap), then moved daily → hourly on the free `:40` offset. `clean-stale-git.sh` gained an age-escalation section (`WORKTREE_ESCALATE_DAYS=14`) that emits `NEEDS-DISPOSITION:` lines for stale **unmerged** worktrees — surfaced for a human call, never auto-deleted, since an unmerged branch holds work that exists nowhere else — and a machine-readable `SUMMARY:` line that `keep-work-flowing-cc` § 2 now tails, so a reaper that stops running shows up in the briefing within the hour instead of after eleven days. **The ticket's own Done-when found a bug bigger than the ticket.** Running the reaper twice to prove two consecutive greens produced `deleted branch: claude/festive-hofstadter-38be3b` — the branch checked out in the live session executing the ticket. The chain: a session rebased to zero unique commits with nothing uncommitted is byte-for-byte indistinguishable from merged, work-free debris, so run 1 called `git worktree remove --force` on it; the directory was held by a live shell, so removal *partially* succeeded — git unregistered the worktree and deleted `.git`, but the `rm` failed — and because the call is written `… 2>/dev/null && echo`, the failure logged nothing at all; run 2 then recomputed `CHECKED_OUT`, found the worktree absent, and deleted the branch that `is_checked_out` had been protecting. The worktree also degraded into a plain subdirectory of the home repo, so every subsequent `git` command resolved against the home tree — one `git commit` from the exact write THR-672 exists to prevent. Fixed with a `WORKTREE_MIN_IDLE_MINUTES=180` liveness guard (never touch a worktree whose git admin dir shows recent activity), the same guard on the orphan-dir sweep via directory mtime (that sweep would otherwise `rm -rf` a live session's working directory outright), and a loud `WARN: worktree removal FAILED (in use?)` so a partial removal can never again read as a clean run. Verified: a freshly created live worktree survives a full pass, as does the corpse directory of the one this incident broke. **This also settles the three-policy conflict THR-674 flagged over `.claude/worktrees/`** — the reaper's predicate is *merge-gated*, not age-based, which is why it may safely own that directory where `pull-work` Step 0's age-based sweep may not; the two are different sweepers, not a contradiction, and the real defect was always the missing liveness check. Escalation verified against the six genuinely stale worktrees, including the two unmerged docs branches THR-674 named. 12623 tests green (839 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` green. Browser-verify exempt: infra only, no runtime UI. Impediment #188 records the incident. Closes via `Fixes THR-673`.

✅ **THR-675 — Frictionless PRs: the skill-sync false positive dies, auto-merge becomes the closeout** (2026-07-21, Continuous Improvement) — Two halves, one already done and one three weeks overdue. **Repo settings were already correct:** `allow_auto_merge` and `delete_branch_on_merge` both read `true`, so scope items 1-setting and 3 needed verification, not change; the real work was documenting `gh pr merge --auto --merge` as the standard closeout in `pull-work` (new "Closeout — ship with auto-merge, don't poll CI" section), the `tb-opus-pickup` scheduled-task prompt, and the CLAUDE.md Definition of Done, so executor sessions stop burning 3–8 min per ship in a CI poll loop. The gate is deliberately untouched — branch protection and the required `Test · Typecheck · Build` check stay, per the H6 verdict in `Docs/plans/2026-07-20-git-cicd-clean-delivery.md`, which kept the PR gate precisely because it held a phantom 3,379-line reversal (THR-614's rename among it) out of `main` for days. Auto-merge removes the waiting, not the gate; a red check simply means the PR never merges and the next hourly run resumes the issue via the Step 1.7 upstream-shipped check. **The hook fix is the substantive half.** `scripts/check-skill-sync.js` walked both skill trees straight off the filesystem, so a **gitignored** artifact — canonically an `image-manipulation-workspace` `*.png`, caught by the blanket `*.png` rule in `.gitignore` — sitting under `.claude/skills/` but not the `.agents/` mirror registered as `missing_in_agents` drift and exited 1, blocking **every** commit including ones touching zero skill files. That is the single most-recurring impediment in the log: ~63 mentions across #118, #124, #125, #132, #146, #186, worked around for three weeks with `:sync`-first, `git clean -Xfd`, or `--no-verify` (which bypasses the gate wholesale). `walkFiles` now filters against an ignored-path set built from one batched `git check-ignore --stdin` call over both trees, memoized so the hook still spawns exactly one git process. Verified across three cases before commit: a gitignored scratch PNG under a shared skill now **passes** (previously exit 1); a content mismatch in a tracked `SKILL.md` still **fails**; a tracked file missing from the mirror still **fails**. Fail-soft per NFP #4 — if `git check-ignore` errors or git is absent, the set is empty and every file is compared, i.e. the pre-THR-675 behaviour, never a crash. The hook also got faster (268ms → ~165ms) because ~69 scratch PNGs are no longer byte-compared. **Ticket-hygiene finding:** the durable-fix ticket for this, THR-540, was filed 2026-06-30 and auto-closed to Done **two minutes later** with no code landing — PR #437's title, "…file THR-540 durable fix", tripped Linear's bare `fix THR-XX` substring auto-close (`[[feedback_linear_autoclose_phrasing]]`), so the log kept accruing recurrences against a ticket the board showed as solved. Impediment #187 records the chain as resolved and names that mechanism. 12623 tests green (839 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` green. Tooling + docs only, zero `src/` — browser-verify exempt: infra only, no runtime UI. Closes via `Fixes THR-675`.

✅ **THR-616 — Mortal Economy P2a: cargo web + first divine verbs** (2026-07-21, M3: Dynamic Economy) — Re-scoped from the full P2 after three checkpoints without a ship: the five seams were cleanly separable, so route-event encounter seeds and cargo-aware tooltips split out as THR-669/THR-670 and this ships the engine+content core already built and tested on branch. **Cargo manifest:** `CargoManifest` plus an additive `manifest` property on `TradeRouteProperties`, derived at route formation from the two endpoints' P1 stock tiers (`buildRouteManifest`) — a `trades_with` edge now names what actually moves instead of one abstract good. The legacy `goodsType` stays readable and becomes the manifest's primary good, and `readCargoManifest` is fail-soft in both directions (legacy → single-good manifest; neither → empty), so no existing route breaks. Constants `ROUTE_MANIFEST_MAX_GOODS=4`, `ROUTE_EXPORT_QUANTITY_FLOOR=60`, `ROUTE_WANT_QUANTITY_CEIL=30`, `ROUTE_BALANCE_SCORE_DIVISOR=3`; assignment traced as `route_cargo_assigned`. **Route-formation bias consumed:** `computeRouteFormationBias()` folds `ROUTE_FORMATION_BALANCE_BIAS=0.25 × scoreRoutePairBalance()` into the target-specific `worldImpact` component in `strategicActionCandidates.ts`, gated on `create_trade_route` and fail-soft on a missing source node (source resolved via `getAgentLocationId()`, the same `located_at` node `createTradeRoute` uses). The constant and the scoring helper had shipped in slice 1 but nothing read them — merchants now prefer complementary surplus↔scarce partners, so the trade web that grows is about the map's real scarcities rather than proximity alone. **First two economic verbs:** graph-executor ops `bless_harvest`/`blight_harvest` (auto-routed via `graphOnlyOps`, the THR-605/THR-611 pattern — graph+ctx only, no `GameState` needed) shift every **staple** resource's quantity at the target location by `LOC_BLESS_HARVEST_STOCK_DELTA`/`LOC_BLIGHT_STOCK_DELTA`=25, clamped to [0,100]. No `touchWorld()` in the op itself: the stock-tier phase already touches on a *tier* change (the `locationSubtype` precedent), so touching on the raw quantity write would over-invalidate. Tier re-derives next tick in `phaseResourceStockTiers`, which is the point — the consequence lands a beat later as *the world noticing* a good year or a bad one, not as a stat popping under the cursor. This extends the shipped `loc.bless_harvest` (THR-401, which only moved prosperity/health) with its economic leg and adds the missing inverse `loc.blight`; both carry `technicalEffect` (THR-604) and effects-line prose (THR-639). Surfaced via the essence-source milestone beat's `grantsActionIds` alongside `loc.open_markets` — deliberately **not** `starter:true`, which under the empty THR-501 starter floor would have left them shipped-but-unreachable (the exact `loc.open_markets` orphan case). 7 fail-soft tests cover staple-only targeting, the 0/100 clamps, tier lift, and the no-staples / missing-location paths. **Rulebook:** new §10 "The World at Work" gives the economy a rules-of-play synthesis — resource classes and the scarce/adequate/surplus tier vocabulary, the prosperity coupling weight, the Livelihood line, manifests, the complementarity bias, and the two verbs — with honest `[IMPL]` flags and `[DESIGN]` flags naming the four real gaps (route-event seeds THR-669, cargo tooltips THR-670, faction economic power THR-617, army supply coupling THR-626). Former §10 "Winning and Losing" renumbered → §11. The quick-reference card gained a matching block: the plan doc ties the card update to the divine economic verbs landing, and a 2026-07-04 scope amendment moved them up from P4 into this phase, so the trigger fired here. 12623 tests green (839 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` green, 30-tick CLI smoke clean (389 agents, 57 events, no throw) after rebasing 67 commits onto current `main`. Engine + content + docs with zero `src/components`/`views`/`index.css` — browser-verify exempt: no runtime UI touched, and the one surface that would need a WebGL pass (cargo-aware route tooltips) is deliberately THR-670. Closes via `Fixes THR-616`.

✅ **THR-634 — Retire Codex-agent remnants** (archived from project-status 2026-07-21) — Continuous Improvement) — deleted the last tracked scaffolding of the executor retired by THR-486: `codex.md` + `.ai-codex/` (`components.md`, `lib.md`, all carrying a stale codesight snapshot claiming 370 importers for `graph.ts` vs the current 531), the three `CODEX_*` computer-use env candidates and the `CODEX_INTERNAL_ORIGINATOR_OVERRIDE` branch in `scripts/session-precheck.ts` (status-equivalent to the fallthrough it preceded — `cu: unknown` either way), the now-orphaned `.ai-codex` entry in `rebuild-index.ts`'s `SKIP_DIRS`, the dead `'Codex review'` required coordination key in `lint-plan-doc.ts` (the live protocol requires only Suggested model / Parallel-safe with / Mutex with, so it flagged every current-protocol plan doc), and the stale "Ready for Dev/Codex" queue reference in `.claude/commands/intent-judge.md`. **Scope correction:** the ticket's item 4 (`drift-scan/index.ts` `"codex"`) was **kept** — its stated conditional ("if it only served Codex-lane detection") is false: line 78 is a `UL_STOPWORDS` entry consumed at line 612 to suppress undeclared-UL-term flagging, and 7 `src/` files still carry the word via the in-game Codex UI, so removing it would have manufactured a false positive. `scripts/retro-draft.ts`'s `codex_home` matcher also kept (it categorizes pre-existing historical impediment text). All explicit keeps honored (`src/components/Codex/**`, `?view=codex`, playtest-interface rubric, historical plan docs, `.codesight/`). Full suite **12512 green (832 files)**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, vite build green, precheck fingerprint still prints. Scripts + docs only, zero `src/` → browser-verify exempt. Closes via `Fixes THR-634`.

✅ **THR-672 — The home tree becomes an inert, self-healing mirror of `main`** (2026-07-21, Continuous Improvement) — Three actors were mutating a tree that is supposed to be a read-only mirror: the CC harness (an hourly plumbing update of `refs/heads/main` plus a bare `git checkout HEAD` that parked the tree detached three mornings running — no agent transcript anywhere contains that command, so it is harness-level and **not fixable from the repo**), scheduled sessions running checkout/commit branch dances directly in the home tree, and `main` moving underneath a checked-out tree, which manufactures phantom "staged" diffs that read as catastrophic damage. **Autosync reattach:** `threadbare-autosync.ps1` gains a step 1.5 that runs *before* the branch guard — a detached HEAD is precisely the state the old guard would skip on forever — and self-heals the one provably loss-free park: detached ∧ 0 unique commits ∧ 0 tracked modifications → `git switch main`, logged as `reattached: was parked at <sha>, nothing stranded`, then straight into the normal fast-forward. Both conditions are named constants. Every other detached state moves nothing and emits one loud `MANUAL REPAIR NEEDED` line carrying the recipe correct for *that* case — the stash/switch/pull block when only tracked dirt blocks it, and an explicit "Do NOT reset" with `git log origin/main..HEAD --oneline` when unique commits exist, because handing a reset recipe to a tree holding commits that exist nowhere else is the one mistake that actually loses work. The never-destructive contract holds: the only ref move is a `git switch` gated on nothing being stranded. **Home-tree git rule:** `keep-work-flowing-cc` and `flush-plan-docs` (both repo copies, both `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/` pointers) now forbid `checkout`/`switch`/`commit`/`merge`/`rebase`/`reset` with the home tree as CWD; access is read-only `git -C` queries and file reads, and all branch/commit/push work moves to the session's own worktree — branches are repo-global and push works from any worktree, so relocating the write side costs nothing. `flush-plan-docs` needed real surgery rather than a warning, since the docs it publishes exist *only* in the home tree: new **Step 2c.5** relocates the doc byte-for-byte into the session worktree, scope guards re-point at `$HOME_TREE`, and the direct-to-main push path is removed (the commit now lands on the flush branch, so pushing `main` would not carry it). **Verified** by running the patched script against four purpose-built scratch repos covering every guard branch — reattach+FF, tracked-dirty refusal, unique-commit refusal, and the unchanged on-main fast-forward. Deferred as THR-678: a published plan doc leaves an untracked home-tree copy that blocks autosync's fast-forward, and the obvious fix (deleting it from the flush lane) is the same class of home-tree mutation this ticket exists to reduce. 12596 tests green (838 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` green. Browser-verify exempt: infra only, no runtime UI. Closes via `Fixes THR-672`.

✅ **THR-671 — Freshness signal distinguishes "parked off-branch" from "behind"** (2026-07-21, Continuous Improvement) — The home tree sat DETACHED at a frozen snapshot for days while local `main` was 0/0 with `origin/main`. Every hourly briefing reported "N commits behind and climbing" (58 → 79) because the ping measured `HEAD..origin/main` on a parked HEAD — arithmetically true, semantically false. Agents read the climbing number as decay and escalated for days; the actual repair was two commands. **Precheck:** `probeBranchStaleness()` returned a bare `detached` key with zero guidance, so the detached branch now runs `git rev-list --count origin/main..HEAD` and splits on the result — `parked-at-ancestor` (0 unique, carries the safe `git stash push -m home-tree-recovery && git switch main && git pull --ff-only origin main` repair) vs `parked-with-unique-commits:N` ("do NOT reset", hand the SHAs to a session). A behind-count is never emitted for a detached HEAD. **Fail-soft:** if the unique count cannot be read, it falls back to the old `detached`/unknown key rather than advertising a repair it cannot prove is loss-free — the one case where staying silent beats being helpful. **Briefing ping:** `keep-work-flowing-cc` § 2 now measures three independent things with deliberately distinct wording — (a) `main..origin/main` for autosync health, (b) HEAD-vs-main parked state expressed by unique count and never by a behind-count, (c) tracked-dirty vs untracked counts, since only tracked dirt blocks autosync and untracked files are a note, not an alarm. **CLAUDE.md:** the freshness bullet listed only `behind:*`/`stale-branch:*`/`unknown`, leaving the key an agent actually hit unmapped; replaced with the exhaustive seven-key table plus the repair block, and it now permits an agent to *run* the repair on `parked-at-ancestor` instead of hard-stopping — the precheck has already proven nothing authored can be lost. 12596 tests green (838 files) including 6 new ones covering both detached cases, the fail-soft fallback, and a regression guard asserting the 79-commit behind-count never resurfaces for a parked HEAD; `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` green. Verified live against real git in a detached worktree (`freshness=parked-with-unique-commits:1`), and the attached-branch path confirmed unregressed. Browser-verify exempt: tooling/docs only, no runtime UI. Closes via `Fixes THR-671`.

✅ **THR-653 — Cowork cutover: CC scheduled-task lane becomes the real registry** (2026-07-20, Pure Claude Code Migration) — Phase 3 of the migration, unblocked when THR-652's parallel-run gate closed GO. **Two documented-but-fictional tasks made real:** the CLAUDE.md table had listed `weekly-retro` and `weekly-memory-grooming` as live CC-lane tasks, but neither had ever been registered with the scheduler — the retro entry even carried a "create this once" snippet that nobody had run. Both now exist and are armed (`0 17 * * 5` fires ~17:09; `3 16 * * 0` fires ~16:10), the memory-grooming prompt ported from its on-disk copy with the Cowork leg demoted to an expected fail-soft miss. **`keep-work-flowing-cc` moved :20 → :45**, taking over the slot the Cowork PM task vacates (cron `45 * * * *`, fires ~:53:13). **Table reconciled against `list_scheduled_tasks`:** `flush-plan-docs` is registered `0 * * * *` (fires ~:03:49), not the documented `15 * * * *`; the table now splits live-CC / GitHub-Actions / Cowork-pending-disable / never-created, and records cron *and* observed fire time after the jitter, since slot name, cron minute and fire time are three different numbers and conflating them is what let the drift hide. **Verified limit:** the four Cowork tasks cannot be reached from CC — invisible to `list_scheduled_tasks`, no `SKILL.md` on disk, and a sweep of `local-agent-mode-sessions/**/.claude/tasks/` returns 3,088 files that are all per-run subagent transcripts, not task definitions. Their disable is a Christian-owned switch, written up in plain language as item 2 of `Design/user-actions.md` with the one real catch flagged: `keep-work-flowing` is safe to kill now, but the other three have no replacement yet, and `daily-backlog-grooming` is what keeps the queue moving. **Deferred deliberately (THR-677):** `daily-backlog-grooming` + `weekly-workflow-retro` prompts are unrecoverable, and authoring a standing queue-mutating task from its documented purpose rather than its actual prompt was judged the wrong call for an unattended lane; `weekly-project-hygiene`'s 201-line prompt audits `.agents/skills/`, the THR-192 skill-sync hook and the two-queue model, all of which THR-654 deletes, so porting it now would be writing content to be immediately invalidated. Docs/config only, no `src/` touched — browser-verify exempt. Closes via `Fixes THR-653`.

✅ **THR-665 — Thread-tug attend badge replaces the map vibration** (2026-07-19, Attention Tier Model) — Thread tugs are the shaping-tier "something is about to happen" signal. Their only presentation was a reach-coloured vibration on the map's thread lines and their only interaction was clicking the tugged agent on the map, which silently spent attention; Christian played 30 turns without ever perceiving the vibration. **Vibration removed end-to-end:** the `TugData` type, the `activeTugData` memo in GameView, the `activeTugs` prop through HexMapV2, and `ThreadLineMesh`'s `setActiveTugs` + vibrate constants/branch are all deleted — thread lines render statically, and the now-unused `REACH_ICON_COLORS` import went with them. **Tug badge on the thread row:** new pure `threadTugBadgeModel` selector groups unattended tugs by row (most-urgent-by-`curationScore` wins, newest breaks ties) and `ThreadTugBadge` renders them as a `∿` badge — deliberately a sibling of the THR-664 `EncounterBadge`, same `IconButton` + `Tooltip` primitives in a distinct state, because one says "something happened" and the other "something is about to". Threat level tints across existing semantic tokens only (moderate `--accent-near-miss` → hard `--warning` → deadly `--negative`, no hardcoded hex); count label collapses to `9+`; the pool is a selector input so an unaffordable tug renders dimmed + `aria-disabled`. **The hidden charge is gone:** the attend logic moved out of `handleThreadNodeSelect` into `attendThreadTug`, invoked only by the badge, and the tooltip/aria-label state the price before the click (`formatAttendCost` rounds for display so float noise like `1.2000000000000002` can't surface, while the affordability check keeps the exact value). Attending now also calls `touchWorld` — the old path mutated `attentionPool` in place without bumping `worldVersion`, so the pool readout memo served a stale value. Browser-verified against a live seeded run: badge renders with `data-tug-badge-threat=hard`, a badge click yields `attended:true` + pool `6 → 5.25` (exactly `computeAttendCost('hard','the_first')`) and clears the badge, while a plain row click leaves both pool and tug untouched; zero console errors. 12559 tests green (835 files) incl. 25 new, `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` green, `tsc -b` net-new 0 (3535=3535). No `src/engine/` change → engine smoke not triggered. Closes via `Fixes THR-665`.

✅ **THR-668 — Central interrupt auto-pause** (2026-07-19, Small manual tweaks) — The sim now pauses behind every blocking interrupt modal and resumes only when all are closed, per Christian's report that the world kept running behind encounter/action-card modals. Root causes verified in-browser: `AscendantBeatModal` (incl. the onboarding spine's action-card grants) and `EmergenceDilemmaModal` had no pause wiring, and the five existing per-modal pause effects resumed per-modal — closing the encounter veil restarted the sim under a still-open beat modal. New `useInterruptAutoPause` hook + a single derived `interruptModalOpen` registry in GameView (veil, meeting, premonition, vignette, story beat, beat modal, choice set, emergence dilemma); manual pause survives modal churn; `forceResumeAfterInterruptsRef` preserves commit-and-continue's forced resume. `__DEBUG.getOpenModals()` gains the beat/choice/dilemma surfaces, `getActiveUIState()` gains `simRunning`. 12537 tests green (833 files), build green, browser-verified end-to-end. Closes via `Fixes THR-668`.

✅ **THR-664 — Entity-anchored encounter notifications** (2026-07-19, Attention Tier Model) — Encounter activity now renders on the entity's persistent thread-row card instead of a global transient toast queue, per Christian's directive. A pending `EncounterNotification` becomes a clickable badge on that agent's `CompactThreadRow`: `!` for a live beat, `✦` for unread aftermath, tinted by outcome band (map extracted from ToastStack into `outcomeBandAccent.ts` so badge and toast share one table), with a count when several collapse onto one row and a Tooltip carrying the same `encounterName` + step-meta strings the retired toast card used. Clicking opens the TieredEncounterModal via the existing `selectEncounterRuntimeForNotification` path and marks that notification viewed so the badge clears. **Toasts retired end-to-end:** `encounterToasts` dropped from the GameView merge, `useEncounterNotifications` deleted (meta-builder moved into the new pure `encounterBadgeModel` selector), and the orchestrator's `encounter_completed` / `encounter_step_failure` events no longer carry `notification:{channel:'toast'}` — the events themselves stay, feeding the event log. **Multi-participant anchoring:** new optional `EncounterNotification.participantIds`, populated by `resolveEncounterParticipantIds` (actor plus an actor-typed target), lets one notification badge both threaded rows without being duplicated per agent. **Row slimmed** per the user addendum — agent location line, encounter-pool button and action chip removed, all three already reachable in the agent detail panel (`ThreadDetailView`'s Encounter Pool section verified a superset, including the ranked-options list); priority pip, activity icon, strategic badge, zoom, sphere icon and aspect badge kept. `handleOpenEncounterFromNotification` now returns whether it actually opened, so a stale notification no longer clears its badge with nothing shown. Design-system conformant — built on the shared `IconButton` badge-count pattern + `Tooltip`, all colours via tokens, no new primitive needed. 12534 tests green (833 files), vite build green, `tsc -b` net −1 vs baseline (3535 vs 3536, zero new), 30-tick CLI smoke clean. Browser-verified with badge render, click-to-open, aftermath state, two-participant anchoring, zero console errors. Closes via `Fixes THR-664`.

✅ **THR-634 — Retire Codex-agent remnants** (2026-07-19, Continuous Improvement) — Removed the last tracked scaffolding of the Codex executor retired by THR-486 (2026-06-23), per the user directive "retire all old stuff and all codex stuff". **Deleted:** `codex.md` (root orientation doc) and `.ai-codex/` (`components.md`, `lib.md`) — all three carried a stale codesight snapshot claiming 370 importers for `graph.ts` against the current 531, actively misinforming any agent that read them. **Stripped:** the three `CODEX_*` entries from `COMPUTER_USE_ENV_CANDIDATES` in `scripts/session-precheck.ts` (the `OPENAI_*` pair left intact, per the ticket's explicit remove-list) and the `CODEX_INTERNAL_ORIGINATOR_OVERRIDE` branch, which returned `status:"unknown"` — identical to the fallthrough it immediately preceded, so only the detail string changed; the `.ai-codex` entry in `rebuild-index.ts`'s `SKIP_DIRS`, orphaned by the directory deletion; and `'Codex review'` from `lint-plan-doc.ts`'s `REQUIRED_COORDINATION_KEYS`, a retired-protocol key that made the advisory plan-doc lint flag every plan doc written under the current single-executor coordination block (Suggested model / Parallel-safe with / Mutex with). **Wording only:** `lint-worktree-orphan.ts` header comment ("Codex/Claude worktree" → "Claude Code worktree") and `.claude/commands/intent-judge.md` ("Allow → Ready for Dev/Codex transition" → "Ready for Dev transition"). **Ticket item 4 deliberately not actioned — its conditional was false:** the ticket scoped `scripts/drift-scan/index.ts` line 78's `"codex"` for removal *"if it only served Codex-lane detection"*; grep showed it is a `UL_STOPWORDS` member consumed at line 612 to suppress undeclared-UL-term flagging, sitting beside `claude`/`linear`/`github`/`mcp`, and 7 `src/` files still carry the word through the in-game Codex UI — removing it would have manufactured a drift-scan false positive. `scripts/retro-draft.ts`'s `codex_home` matcher kept on the same reasoning (it categorizes pre-existing historical impediment-log text, not live lane state). All explicit keeps honored: `src/components/Codex/**` and `?view=codex` (in-game UI), playtest-interface rubric mentions, the historical `2026-04-19` / `2026-04-27` Codex plan docs, `.codesight/`. Full suite 12512 green (832 files), `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` green, and `session-precheck.ts` verified still printing its fingerprint after the edit. Scripts + docs only, zero `src/` → browser-verify exempt. Closes via `Fixes THR-634`.

✅ **THR-656 — Art for `loc.open_markets` ("Awning Unfurled")** (2026-07-19, Action System & Unlocks) — Authored the missing card art for the Gold economy action that THR-613 Slice 2b's milestone beat grants, and registered `/assets/actions/awning-unfurled.jpg` in both parallel art maps (`ActionCard.tsx`, `codexRegistry.ts`). Order-sphere direction per STYLE.md: a #d4af37 tessellated lattice unfurling like an awning over a waking market square, twilight base at 10-30% brightness, 1376x768 JPG matching the sibling format. Browser-verified at 1920x1080 via `__DEBUG.fireBeat` — the unlock-reveal card face now renders art instead of the generic placeholder. Cosmetic only; no logic touched.

✅ **THR-607 — UL-proposal: canonized Chapter, Chapter Record, Chapter Ledger** (2026-07-19, Attention Tier Model) — Added the three THR-603 terms to the Ubiquitous Language Encounters shard (`Docs/ubiquitous-language/Encounters.md`), closing the live terminology drift where THR-603 shipped these player- and code-facing names into canon docs before the glossary defined them. **Chapter** = the reading unit of a resolved/in-progress encounter; **Chapter Record** (`ChapterRecord`, `src/types/chapterRecord.ts`) = the post-`enrichProse()` snapshot appended to `gameState.chapterArchive` before the resolved-action prune; **Chapter Ledger** (`ChapterLedger`, IA surface `game.chapter-ledger`) = the always-readable list merging active encounters and resolved chapters. ✅ **THR-605 — COMPLETE: all six no-op ascendant actions wired (final Slice 4 `plant_trap`)** (2026-07-19, Action System & Unlocks) — The last of the six previously-no-op ascendant verbs (`artifact.attune`/`nullify`/`curse`, `loc.fortify`, `sub.trap`, `sub.vision`) now has a real, consumed effect. **Slice 4 — `sub.trap` (`plant_trap`):** the one op needing full `GameState` (it mutates `state.pendingEncounterSeeds`), so unlike the graph-executor-case slices 1–3 it rides the **resolution-intercept path** in `unifiedActionResolution.ts` (mirrors `imbue_item`/`bestow_power`). `applyPlantTrap` (`ascendantExpression.ts`) resolves the intended victim present at the trapped sublocation (or, failing that, on its hex — hex-granular, excludes the acting ascendant, deterministic id-sort) and plants a `PendingEncounterSeed` for the authored `encounter.trap.sprung` beat against them; `evaluateEncounterSeeds` then spawns a real, failable negative encounter whose harm lands through its `condition`-weighted reward pools — the same seed→spawn substrate the faction governance verbs use (no bespoke harm op). **Substrate correction (grep-verified):** the encounter-seed pathway has no hex-arrival gate (a seed spawns for its `targetAgentId` the moment it's eligible, wherever they stand), so the plan's "no targetAgentId, fires on the next agent to occupy the hex" was unreachable without net-new machinery — seeded against a present victim instead. Systemic-wiring-guide 6-op aggregation added (both wiring paths documented side by side). The two residual no-ops (`sub.sanctify`/`sub.sanctify_tavern`) are out of this issue's 6-action scope (flagged by THR-604) → follow-up **THR-662**. 7 new fail-soft tests + 27 targeted green, `vite build` green, `tsc -b` net-new 0 (3542=3542), 30-tick CLI smoke clean (389 agents). Engine + content-data only → browser-verify exempt. Closes via `Fixes THR-605`.

✅ **THR-576 — Docs/plans generated index** (2026-07-18, Continuous Improvement) — New `scripts/rebuild-plans-index.ts` generates `Docs/plans/INDEX.md`, an exhaustive deterministic catalog of every top-level plan doc (544 plans across 6 months + 2 undated), one line each: date · linked title · linked Linear issue · declared status, with a ⚠️ *superseded by* marker. Build tooling + docs only, zero `src/` touched; full suite 12481 green (828 files), `vite build` green, `tsc -b` net-new 0. Closes via the `Fixes THR-576` keyword.

Fail-soft (coalesces missing fields; a granted-set throw degrades to a loud `warning`, never crashes); never runs in the tick loop; detects only, grants nothing.
